| 441 | ✅ Done | Now | P1 | `feature` | **Career 360 chat tools failed with "Resume content is too short or empty" — segment / gap clicks navigated to `/chat?q=…` so the panel had no resumeId** (2026-05-07) closed 2026-05-07 | Production user report at `https://hired.video/tools/career-360` → clicking the Narrative segment took the user to `/chat?q=…`, the chat panel ran summarize / scorer / sentiment / extractor against empty input and returned `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` from `/api/resume/summarize`. Root cause: `AIChatPanel.tsx::activeResumeIdFromPath` was the only resume-id resolver, and Career 360 didn't put a resume in the URL. Fix shipped in three pieces: (a) **AIChatPanel resolver renamed** `activeResumeIdFromPath` → `resolveActiveResumeId(pathname, pageContext)` — falls back to `pageContext.resume?.id` when the path has nothing, so any future tool surface that opens chat with a `ResumeContext` automatically binds resume-bound tools without re-implementing the rule. (b) **Career360.tsx** now loads the seeker's master resume on mount via `resumeService.getMasterResumeGroups()`, replaces every `navigate("/chat?q=…")` with `useAI().openAIWithPageContextAndMessage(ctx, prompt)` where `ctx = createResumeContext("resume-view", { id, title, summary }, role)`. The priming message embeds the chosen Career 360 target so the LLM knows the role even without scrolling. Single helper `openCoach(basePrompt)` is the DRY entry point — segment clicks, gap clicks, and any future chat-launcher route through it. (c) **TargetRoadmap.tsx** stripped its own `useNavigate`/`navigate("/chat?q=…")` and now raises `onCoachGap(gap)` so the parent owns chat-context construction. Layout: moved the target picker INTO the wheel card under the wheel SVG (separator above) so the whitespace under the wheel is filled instead of pushing the picker into a separate full-width card below the right-sidebar. |

| 445 | ✅ Done | Now | P1 | `arch` | **JSON schema validation lived OUTSIDE the LLM model fallback loop — a single bad response from the primary model killed the whole call even though 10+ free models on OpenRouter were still in the chain** (2026-05-09) closed 2026-05-09 | Production user report at `https://hired.video/tools/career-360` → POST `/api/career360/select` returned `503 AI_UNAVAILABLE` even though three providers (OpenRouter / Cerebras / Ollama) plus 11 OpenRouter free models were configured for the `career_360` use case. Root cause: `services/adapters/ai.ts::callLLMJsonWithUsage` ran `JSON.parse` + Zod validation AFTER `callLLMWithUsage` returned. `tryProviderChain`'s per-model loop only retried on transport / HTTP failures (429, timeout, empty content) — when qwen3-coder:free emitted prose-with-fences or a schema-violating object, the validation throw escaped the loop and no other model in the chain was tried. Fixed by threading an optional `validateContent(content)` callback through `tryProviderChain` so JSON-mode callers can fail a model on parse / schema errors and advance to the next model in the same call (instead of bubbling out). `callLLMJsonWithUsage` now passes a closure that strips fences, parses, and Zod-validates; `callLLMWithUsageAndValidator` is the internal seam. Also bumped `career_360` to `maxTokens: 4000` with cross-provider fallbacks to qwen3-next + gemma-4-31b — even a single profile now retries across 11 OpenRouter models for schema validity, and the cross-provider chain is the safety net. Route's catch-all now distinguishes `AI_INVALID_RESPONSE` (schema-validation exhaustion across the entire chain) from `AI_UNAVAILABLE` (transport outage). Affects every `callJson` site (~30 routes), all of which become more resilient automatically. |

| 473 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `quality` | **`job_items.updatedAt` does not auto-bump on UPDATE — Drizzle's `defaultNow()` only fires on insert** (2026-05-09) | Identified while building the job-body-cleanse worker (#474). The cleanse worker's "re-cleanse on edit" path queries `WHERE bodyCleansedAt IS NULL OR bodyCleansedAt < updatedAt`, so it only re-inspects edited rows if the writer explicitly bumped `updatedAt`. This is not guaranteed across job_items writers (scraper.ts, JobService, manual-post route, sentiment-score updates, etc.). First-pass cleanse over `IS NULL` works fine, so existing HTML gets fixed; re-cleanse on later edits is unreliable. Fixing unblocks: the cleanse worker noticing post-cleanse user edits, plus any other table that wants change-detection cursors. Options: (a) Drizzle `.$onUpdate(() => new Date())` on every `updatedAt` column, (b) Postgres trigger on UPDATE, (c) explicit `updatedAt: new Date()` audit at every write site. |

Per-row cursor `bodyCleansedAt`/`bodyCleanseStatus` on `job_items`; partial index keeps the pending-rows query tight. Admin UI at `/admin/workers` lists every registered worker, drills into run history, and shows per-run failure samples. Migration 0080. |
| 475 | ✅ Done | Now | P2 | `feature` | **Studio music timeline supports multiple overlapping clips — "Use as music" now appends a new layer instead of overwriting, and rendered URL clips actually play in preview + export** (2026-05-09) closed 2026-05-09 | `VideoAudio` gained an optional `clips: AudioClip[]` field; `frontend/lib/audio/normalize-clips.ts` is the single source of truth that hoists legacy `audio.track` into a one-clip list so old projects keep working unchanged. New shared scheduler `frontend/lib/audio/clip-scheduler.ts` handles both procedural preset clips (`AmbientPlayer` instances bracketed by `setTimeout`) and rendered URL clips (`AudioBufferSourceNode.start(when, offset, duration)` for sample-accurate scheduling). Both `VideoPreview` (live playback at the playhead) and `video-exporter` (render mix) consume that one scheduler — DRY for the entire music-mixing path. `StudioActions.handleMusicReady` now drops each rendered WAV at the current playhead as a new clip with its real duration. `NLETimeline.AudioLaneRow` was extracted into a `LaneSegmentBlock` component that self-gates on `seg.editable` — music clips get drag-to-move, edge-drag to trim, and an × to delete; recording / voiceover segments stay derived + read-only. Sidebar preset picker writes through the clip model with stable id `sidebar-preset` so re-selecting a preset replaces just that slot and leaves user-rendered layers in place. Side benefit: rendered URL clips played silently before this pass — `AmbientPlayer.play("https://…")` looked up `PRESETS["https://…"]` and bailed out — so single-track custom music wasn't audible in preview or export despite the timeline showing it. The new scheduler fixes that too. |

| 477 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `quality` | **Music-clip drag rebuilds the live-preview audio context on every mouse-move tick** (2026-05-09) | `VideoPreview`'s scheduler keys off `clipsKey` (id|track|startSec|endSec|volume joined). Dragging a clip mutates `startSec` continuously, which changes the key on every render and tears down + re-creates the AudioContext + re-decodes URL buffers. Audible as glitches / silence during drag. Fix options: (a) debounce key changes by ~150ms while a drag is active, (b) detect "same id set, just timing" and patch start times on existing buffer sources via `source.stop()` + new `source.start(...)` rather than rebuild, (c) only rebuild on drag-end. Identified 2026-05-09. |
| 478 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `quality` | **`StoryboardEditor` and the sidebar volume row still read `video.audio.label`/`audio.track` to display the "active music" label — show "Custom" / "None" while user-rendered layers are live** (2026-05-09) | The legacy `track`/`label` fields no longer fully describe the music timeline once `clips[]` is in play. `StoryboardEditor.tsx:384` falls back to "None" even when there are 3 layered clips; the sidebar's "Volume slider — shown when a track is selected" gate (`script.video.audio.track !== "none"`) hides the master volume slider as soon as the user adds a custom layer that clears the legacy `track`. Fix: introduce a `getActiveMusicLabel(audio, totalDuration)` helper alongside `normalizeAudioClips` that returns "Custom (3 layers)" / preset label / "None"; gate the sidebar volume + StoryboardEditor display on `clips.length > 0` rather than `track !== "none"`. Identified 2026-05-09 implementing multi-clip music. |
| 479 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `feature` | **Live-preview music doesn't resync on big seeks — playhead can be 10s into the timeline while music is still 2s in** (2026-05-09) | Initial implementation had a drift-detection effect that tore down + rebuilt the schedule when `currentTime` jumped > 0.5s, but it added churn (effect deps included `currentTime`, firing every RAF tick) and timing edge-cases caused premature rebuilds during normal playback. Removed in this pass — playback now stays in sync as long as the user only Plays/Pauses; scrubbing the playhead while playing leaves the music running on its original schedule. Fix: use `performance.now()` to compute drift independently of `audio ctx.currentTime` (which has its own offset), or expose a "rebuild from this offset" entry on `ClipScheduleHandle` so seeks can transplant rather than tear-down + re-decode. Identified 2026-05-09 simplifying the audio effect. |
| 480 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `feature` | **Music lane width doesn't grow when a clip extends past `script.scenes` total duration — the clip is rendered but visually offscreen** (2026-05-09) | `NLETimeline`'s `totalDuration` is `script.scenes.reduce(...)`, audio extents are not considered. Workaround in this pass: `handleMusicReady` clamps each new clip's `endSec` to `min(rendered duration, totalDuration)` so the layer always fits in the visible lane. Side effects: a 30s render on a 12s video is truncated to 12s of audio at render time (preview + export both honor `endSec`); the user can drag-trim the right edge to extend later, but only up to the scene-derived total. Fix: change the timeline's working width to `max(scenesTotal, max-clip-endSec)` so a longer audio layer extends the rule + scrolls naturally; the playhead RAF should also cap at `scenesTotal` (video stops while music continues, then fades out). Identified 2026-05-09 after the user reported new clips not appearing on the lane (they were appended at `currentTimeSec + 30s` so extended past visible width). |
| 481 | 🔲 Open | Now | P1 | `bug` | **Studio "Generating your storyboard" dialog can hang indefinitely — modal blocks all UI, no abort, no timeout** (2026-05-09) | `POST /api/studio/storyboard` ([api/src/routes/studio.ts:683-757](hired.video/api/src/routes/studio.ts)) awaits `llm.callJson(...)` with no per-call timeout. User reproduced taking **2 minutes 48 seconds** for a Basic-template generation. If the LLM stalls longer (or `tokenLedger.ensureBudget` hangs on a misbehaving DB read), the route blocks until the API client's 360s `NetworkError("Request timeout")` fires. `GenerationProgressDialog` is gated only on `status === "generating"` ([frontend/components/studio/GenerationProgressDialog.tsx:14-15](hired.video/frontend/components/studio/GenerationProgressDialog.tsx#L14-L15)) with `onPointerDownOutside` + `onEscapeKeyDown` preventDefault'd, so the user can't dismiss or retry while it's spinning — they just stare at the spinner for up to 6 minutes before the toast fires. Fix: wrap the LLM call in `Promise.race` with a ~45s timeout that falls through to the existing mock branch ([studio.ts:728-731](hired.video/api/src/routes/studio.ts#L728-L731)); add a "Cancel" button on `GenerationProgressDialog` that aborts via `AbortController` + flips `status` back to `idle`. Unblocks: users in poor-LLM-latency conditions can retry or proceed offline-mock-first. Identified 2026-05-09 — user reported "click Continue, panel shows but never completes" on a fresh storyboard generation. Unrelated to the multi-clip music work in #475. |

The LLM call is then expensive (2.8 minutes in this run) and produces a video that's unusable without a manual title edit. Fix: BasicIntake's description input should preflight-check length (>= 8 chars), repeated-character runs (≤4 same letter), and strip whitespace before allowing Generate to fire. | 485 | ✅ Done | Now | P1 | `bug` | **Save button silently re-ran the AI generator on every click — wiped user edits and burned ~30s+ of LLM time per Save** (2026-05-09) closed 2026-05-09 | `handleSavePipeline` ([StudioLayout.tsx:1628](hired.video/frontend/pages/studio/StudioLayout.tsx#L1628)) was documented as "Generate the script if it's missing OR re-generate if the user clicked Save with content edits since the last script" but actually ran `handleGenerate` whenever the `generate` toggle in the Save dropdown was on (default ON). User reproduced 2026-05-09: clicking Save on a fully-edited 5-scene storyboard popped the "Generating your storyboard" modal for 2.8 minutes and would have overwritten manual scene edits if it had finished. Fix: pulled the auto-skip rule into `describeRunPlan` ([save-pipeline-config.ts:223](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/save-pipeline-config.ts)) via a new `RunPlanContext { hasScript }` parameter — when a script with scenes exists, `generate` is marked `autoSkipped: true` and excluded from `effectiveStepsForSave`. SaveButton + StudioLayout pipeline both pass the same context so the dropdown preview ("Save will: persist + Render video + Upload to library") cannot disagree with what fires. Per-step row in the dropdown shows the auto-skip reason ("Skipped — a storyboard already exists. Save preserves your edits.") and disables the toggle. All 23 save-pipeline-config tests still pass. |

| 487 | ✅ Done | Now | P1 | `bug` | **Studio screen recording: PiP mode acquired the screen but the live preview never rendered — looked like the record panel was missing** (2026-05-09) closed 2026-05-09 | `WebcamRecorder.startPreview` set `videoPreviewRef.current.srcObject = previewStream` BEFORE `setRecordingState("previewing")`, but the `<video ref={videoPreviewRef}>` element is conditionally rendered only when `recordingState !== "idle"` ([WebcamRecorder.tsx:692-694](hired.video/frontend/components/studio/WebcamRecorder.tsx#L692-L694)). Fix: removed the inline `srcObject` assignment in `startPreview`/`startCanvasComposite`, added a `useEffect` keyed on `[recordingState, isPipMode]` that attaches `compositeStreamRef.current ?? webcamStreamRef.current` to `videoPreviewRef` (and `webcamStreamRef` to `pipVideoRef` when PiP) once the elements mount. Also calls `stopAllStreams()` on permission-denied error so a retry from idle starts clean. |

The composite stream's screen audio tracks (added in `startCanvasComposite`) were never touched, so MediaRecorder kept encoding system audio with the mic icon visibly "off". Pre-mute (clicking mute before stream acquired) also had no effect — newly-acquired streams came up hot. Fix: added `applyMuteToAllStreams(muted)` that iterates webcam + screen + composite refs, called from `toggleMute` and from each `acquire*` function. `isMutedRef` mirrors `isMuted` so non-reactive acquisition callbacks can read the latest state without taking it as a dep. |
| 489 | ✅ Done | Now | P3 | `quality` | **Studio screen-only mode prompted for camera even though the camera feed was discarded** (2026-05-09) closed 2026-05-09 | The screen-only branch in `WebcamRecorder.startPreview` called `acquireWebcam(...)` purely to get a mic, but `acquireWebcam` requested `video: { facingMode, width, height }` too — surfacing a camera permission prompt to users who picked "Screen", then throwing the camera track away. Also made the idle viewport copy/icon mode-aware ("Share Screen" + Monitor icon for screen modes; "Click 'Share Screen' to choose a screen and start your webcam" for PiP). |
| 490 | ✅ Done | Now | P3 | `feature` | **Studio screen recording: cancelling the screen picker for PiP mode shows a dead error and forces a manual click to retry** (2026-05-09) closed 2026-05-09 | "Try again" button now shipped inline in the error banner — visible only while `recordingState === "idle"`, calls `startPreview()` directly. | 495 | ✅ Done | Now | P1 | `bug` | **LLM dispatch was burning up to 14 × 45s of OpenRouter sibling-model timeouts before the use case's own cross-provider fallback ran — producing 1.8min API calls** (2026-05-09) closed 2026-05-09 | `tryProviderChain` ([api/src/services/adapters/ai.ts:130](hired.video/api/src/services/adapters/ai.ts#L130)) always walked `[startModel, ...fallbackChainOf(provider)]` — for OpenRouter that's 14 models. When a use case had explicit cross-provider `fallbacks` in `USE_CASE_PROFILES` (e.g. `studio_script` falls Claude Haiku → qwen3-next on OpenRouter), the dispatch in `llm-client.ts` would still let the primary attempt grind through 13 unrelated sibling models on the same vendor before the explicit fallback ran — worst case 14 × 45s = 10.5 minutes per call. User-reported repro: 1.8min on a `studio_script` storyboard call (≈2-3 sibling timeouts). Fix: added a `_dispatch.walkSiblings` flag to `LLMOptions`/`LLMMessagesOptions`. The dispatch facade sets `walkSiblings = false` whenever the use case has cross-provider `fallbacks` (the use-case author has named the recovery path; don't override it with sibling-walking). `tryProviderChain` honors the flag and tries only the pinned model in that case. Single-profile use cases keep the full sibling walk because it's their only recovery. Also dropped OpenRouter `timeoutMs` from 45s → 20s so each attempt fails faster. Net effect for `studio_script`: 1 attempt × 20s timeout (Claude Haiku) + 1 attempt × 20s timeout (qwen fallback) = ~40s ceiling vs the previous ~10.5min. |

Fix: SuperAdmin tool (or scheduled job) that pings every model in the catalog with a 1-token prompt and flags ones returning non-200. Cache health for ~1h so the runtime fallback skips known-down models. Out of scope for the latency fix because the model order also depends on quality, not just availability. Unblocks: confidence that the published catalog matches what's actually serving traffic, and a place for SA to remove rotted models without redeploying. |
| 497 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P3 | `feature` | **No dispatch telemetry — when a call falls back across providers, we don't record which profile actually answered or how many attempts it took** (2026-05-09) | The `console.warn` lines in `tryProviderChain` and the dispatch loop in `llm-client.ts` are the only visibility into fallback events. Without this, we can't tell from production data which use cases are actually leaning on their cross-provider fallback (i.e. their primary model is unreliable in practice) — we just see a slow request. Fix: extend `tokenLedger.charge` with `{ profileChain: string[]; attemptCount: number; totalLatencyMs: number }` so SA can rank "use cases where the primary failed > 10% of the time this week". Out of scope for #495's latency fix; tracked separately because the schema delta touches `ai_usage_records`. Unblocks: data-driven decisions on which use case primaries to swap out. NOTE: largely obsoleted by the planned port to `@seanhogg/builderforce-sdk` (#498) — gateway already returns `_builderforce.{resolvedModel, retries, pool}` per response. |

Dropped `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` / `CEREBRAS_API_KEY` / `OLLAMA_API_KEY` / `LLM_DISPATCH_MODE` from `Env`; kept `BUILDERFORCE_API_KEY` + `BUILDERFORCE_GATEWAY_URL`. Updated SA `/api/admin/impersonate/ai/{use-cases,models,usage}` route to read from `USE_CASE_PROFILES` (our policy) + gateway's live `models.list()` / `usage.get()` — no more local vendor registry. 1436/1436 tests pass. Per-user 1M-token Pro budget stays in our `tokenLedger`; `featureKey` + `toolRunId` + `sessionId` ride to gateway via typed `metadata` and echo back on `_builderforce.metadata` for billing trace-back. | | The SDK is OpenAI-shape over `https://api.builderforce.ai/llm/v1/chat/completions` with server-side vendor failover (response carries `_builderforce.resolvedModel / retries`). Adopting it would delete ~900 LOC of our LLM plumbing: [providers.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/adapters/providers.ts), [openai-compat.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/adapters/openai-compat.ts), most of [ai.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/adapters/ai.ts) (`tryProviderChain`, `callLLMJsonWithUsage`, `callLLMWithUsage`, `callLLMWithMessagesAndUsage`, the `_dispatch.walkSiblings` flag from #495, `MODEL_CATALOG`, `USE_CASE_PROFILES`, `resolveProfileChainForUseCase`, `getProviderApiKey`), and collapse three env vars (`OPENROUTER_API_KEY` / `CEREBRAS_API_KEY` / `OLLAMA_API_KEY`) into one (`BUILDERFORCE_API_KEY`). Our `LLMClient` interface (`callJson` / `callText` / `callMessages`) stays — only the internals swap. **Gating SDK gaps documented in [#499–#501]** (P1 blockers) and [#502–#506] (soft gaps; can port without). Sequence: SDK 0.3.0 lands #499–#501 → Phase 1 ports `callJson`+`callText` (~80% of call sites including `studio_script`) → Phase 2 ports `callMessages` → Phase 3 opts into server-side JSON-schema validation, deletes our schema-retry loop. Unblocks: single SDK upgrade swaps every vendor going forward; gateway-side rate-limit + cost optimization replaces our use-case-profile heuristics; SA dashboard reads from gateway's `/usage` endpoint instead of our `ai_usage_records` (we keep our row for the per-user 1M-token Pro budget which the gateway doesn't know about). |

| 511 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `feature` | **Gateway-internal retry billing semantics unspecified — risk of double-counting tokens against our user-side ledger** (2026-05-09) | The SDK response carries `_builderforce.retries: number` indicating how many times the gateway internally retried before returning a successful response. If the gateway charges the tenant key for each upstream attempt (typical pricing model), the `usage` we record from the final response captures all retries. We then debit that amount once on `tokenLedger.charge` — fine. BUT if the gateway returns 5xx after exhausting its internal retries AND we then advance to the NEXT profile in our cross-vendor chain (which the gateway doesn't know is a logically related call), the tenant has paid for both the failed retry burst AND the new profile's tokens. Our user-side ledger only sees the final successful charge. Result: tenant key spend > sum of user-side debits = gap. Fix: gateway documents whether `usage` on success includes retry tokens (yes/no), and whether failed-but-retried attempts are billed (yes/no). Builderforce SDK could expose `_builderforce.retryUsage[]` so our adapter can choose to debit each gateway retry separately. Until clarified: SA dashboard will under-report user-side spend vs tenant-key spend by the gateway-internal-retry delta. |

| 513 | 🔲 Open | Mid | P3 | `feature` | **Streaming + profile-chain fallback is architecturally incompatible — can't retry once we've started streaming bytes to the user** (2026-05-09, surfaced during contract audit) | None of our 31 call sites stream today, but the studio storyboard generator (#481 — currently 30s spinner) is a candidate. Problem: our `dispatchTextCompletion` walks `[primary, ...fallbacks]` and retries on failure. With streaming we'd commit to the primary's bytes immediately; we can't retry under the user without re-emitting "false" content first. Fix on our side (when streaming work begins): split streaming dispatch into "TTFB race" (try primary, on first-byte timeout fall over to fallback before either has emitted) vs "progressive" (commit to whichever profile produced first byte, no fallback). Tracked here so the architecture decision is recorded — out of scope for the port today, but unblocks any future "stream studio script as it generates" UX. |

For `match` use case (Cerebras-only sub-200ms TTFT requirement, see [providers.ts USE_CASE_PROFILES.match](hired.video/api/src/services/adapters/providers.ts)), substitution to OpenRouter = 50× latency hit the user notices. Fix options: (a) gateway adds `pinModel: true` opt-in that returns 503 instead of substituting, (b) we add post-call substitution alarm in our adapter — when `usage.model !== requested.model` log a SLA-breach event SA can dashboard, (c) we narrow `match` to NOT have a cross-vendor fallback in our chain (since gateway substitutes for us anyway) and accept whatever resolves. (b) is cheapest and gives observability without locking out vendor improvements. (a) is the right long-term contract for latency-pinned use cases. Until decided: silent latency regressions on `match` go unobserved. |

Three duplicate `aiFailure(...)` helpers (career360.ts, profile-audit.ts, match-handlers.ts) collapsed to a single import — the rate-limit-vs-schema branching is now in one file (DRY) and every 503 carries the full `payload: { correlationId, classification, attemptCount, lastAttempt, attempts[] }` so a support ticket has everything SA needs without grepping logs. `attempts[].requestId` correlates to gateway-side logs (closes the operational half of #512). 1436/1436 tests pass. As a result, [use-comm-hub.ts:182](hired.video/frontend/hooks/use-comm-hub.ts#L182) treats every conversation row as already-read (`isRead: (c.unreadCount ?? 0) === 0` always evaluates true), so the inbox bell badge never reflects unread DM count and the Outreach inbox tab can't visually distinguish read/unread message threads. Surfaced (not caused) during gap-#530 P0 work — when external emails light up with real `isRead` state alongside conversations stuck at `isRead: true`, the asymmetry is user-visible. Fix: extend `IConversationsRepository.findConversationsForUser` to LEFT JOIN `messageReadReceipts` and compute unread count per conversation for the requesting user, then thread the result through `buildConversationDtos`. Unblocks: accurate unread badges + visual unread state on DM threads. |

Removed the now-unused `TargetPlanResponseSchema` + `TargetPlanResponse` exports from `shared/career360-types.ts` per the dead-code rule (zero remaining references). |

Callers register `{ name, ingest, coerce }`; the client auto-derives a strict `json_schema` from the lenient Zod schema (via `z.toJSONSchema()`) so the gateway runs vendor-side conformance retry, validates the wire response with the lenient schema inside the per-profile dispatch loop (so a coerce/parse failure also advances the model fallback), and returns the canonical shape from `coerce`. Real-time delivery requires: (Gmail) `users.watch` against a Pub/Sub topic the worker can subscribe to via webhook + 7-day renewal cron — `external_email_accounts.push_expires_at` + the `external_email_accounts_push_expiry_idx` index are already in place for the renewal query; (Outlook) Graph `/subscriptions` POST with a webhook URL + ~70min lifetime renewal cron. Unblocks: sub-minute inbox latency, lower API cost (Push fires only on change vs polling N accounts every 5min). |
| 536 | 🔲 Open | Soon | P2 | `quality` | **Email sync per-message LLM cost is unbounded — full-mailbox first sync on a heavy mailbox can fire hundreds of `email_classify` + `email_summary` calls in one cron tick** (2026-05-10) | [sync.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/email-integration/sync.ts) classifies every new message that wasn't caught by Stage-1 rules, then summarises every job-related message above 0.6. Heavy users (200+ unread inbox) on first connect could fire 100+ LLM calls in a single sync run — no per-user budget gate, no back-pressure, no batch dispatch. Today this is shielded by Stage-1 rules catching the obvious ATS senders, but if the user has lots of recruiter outreach below the rules' precision floor it can spike fast. Fix: (a) pass the user's `JwtPayload` into `runEmailSyncSweep` so per-user token-budget 429s land cleanly, (b) batch the summary stage (one LLM call summarising ≤10 messages at once via `callMessages`), (c) hard-cap per-sync-run classify count and roll the overflow into the next tick. Unblocks: predictable cost on full-mailbox-sync MVP. |
| 537 | 🔲 Open | Mid | P3 | `quality` | **Gmail sync fetches messages serially — bursts >50 messages will time out on a Cloudflare Workers sub-request budget** (2026-05-10) | [providers/gmail.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/email-integration/providers/gmail.ts) walks `users.messages.list` ids and calls `users.messages.get` one-at-a-time. ~300ms per call × 50 = 15s, ~200 messages = a minute, hits the 50-subrequest soft limit and the 30s CPU budget on Workers free tier. Outlook delta endpoint returns full message bodies in pages so it's fine. Fix: parallelise Gmail's `fetchMessage` loop with a small concurrency window (e.g. `Promise.allSettled` × 5), AND use `users.messages.batchGet` (not yet on the public REST API — would need the batch HTTP endpoint with a multipart body) when the count exceeds a threshold. Unblocks: reliable first-sync on heavy mailboxes. |

Fix: shared `providerFetch` wrapper that honours `Retry-After` (single retry, capped delay so a worker invocation doesn't exceed the 30s CPU budget) and downgrades 429 to a `partial` sync rather than an `error`. Unblocks: graceful degradation on transient throttling. |

Backend was returning `TURNSTILE_FAIL` and locking everyone out (extension's "Continue in browser" tab + the in-popup email/password form both hit the same wall). **Mitigation in place (this commit):** [AuthService.handleLogin](hired.video/api/src/services/auth/AuthService.ts) now treats a captcha miss as a soft signal — records the bot_block_event and forces the email-OTP step-up after password verification (`captchaFailed → requiresMfa`). Real account holders prove humanness via inbox; bots without mailbox access stall at the second factor. Extension popup ([shared/login.js](hiredvideo-chrome-extension/shared/login.js) + [shared/login.html](hiredvideo-chrome-extension/shared/login.html)) hosts the OTP entry inline so users don't have to start over in a new tab. **Real fix still owed:** add `VITE_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` to the production frontend build (Cloudflare Pages env vars or `frontend/.env.production`) paired to the backend secret, so the silent challenge runs and only users who fail it get the OTP fallback. **Adjacent gap:** signup is still hard-blocked because [signupGuard.ts](hired.video/api/src/services/auth/signupGuard.ts) hasn't been given the same step-up rail (and adding email-OTP step-up to a NEW user creation flow is a bigger UX call). Unblocks: production sign-in for everyone, and re-enables silent bot defense once the site key ships. |

Two consumers translate it: (i) `applyAnimateEffectsToCtx` walks the chain into canvas ctx state (translate→scale→globalAlpha) which `applyWidgetCanvasState` in [canvas-v2.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/timeline/effects/canvas-v2.ts) calls before rotation/CSS-equivalent phases; (ii) `widgetEffectsToCss()` in [CanvasStage.tsx](hired.video/frontend/components/studio/v2/CanvasStage.tsx) folds the same output into CSS `transform`/`opacity` so the editor preview matches render-side pixel for pixel. `applyWidgetPixelEffects` skips animate kinds so they don't double-apply. Legacy `widget.params.animation` enum + the standalone `LoopAnimationField`/`ANIMATION_OPTIONS` SelectField are gone — image/video widgets carry their Ken-Burns motion entirely in `widget.effects[]` now. Hydration migration in [timeline-emit.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/editor-v2/timeline-emit.ts) (`liftLegacyAnimation`) lifts saved `params.animation` into `animate-*` entries so existing projects don't need a one-shot. V1↔V2 round-trip preserved: `ken_burnsToEffects` in [script-to-canvas.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/editor-v2/script-to-canvas.ts) (V1 import → effect) + `effectsToImageAnimation` in [canvas-to-script.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/editor-v2/canvas-to-script.ts) (V2 → V1 imageAnimation, lossless for the four V1-compat kinds; modern fade/pulse/shake ride only on the effects array). Deleted: `WidgetAnimation` type, `ImageWidgetParams.animation`, `VideoWidgetParams.animation`, `asAnimation`/`normaliseAnimation`/`animationToScript`, `animationFor()` + `studio-v2-*` `@keyframes` in [global.css](hired.video/frontend/global.css), and the legacy synthesis seam in canvas-v2's `widgetEffects()`. |

| 668 | 🔲 Open | Mid | P3 | `quality` | **`computeScriptHash` can't detect asset-URL content drift — if a scene references an R2 image whose contents change at the same URL the hash won't notice and a stale baked video would be served** (2026-05-16 — #665) | [baked-freshness.ts](hired.video/frontend/lib/studio/baked-freshness.ts) `computeScriptHash` hashes the asset URL string, not the asset's content. In practice scene assets are addressed by sha256-prefixed R2 keys so a content change ⇒ URL change; this gap only bites if a future writer mutates an existing R2 object in place. Fix options: (a) include asset Last-Modified / ETag in the hash via a small lookup, (b) enforce immutable R2 keys for all scene-referenced assets (current convention, just not load-bearing). Low likelihood, but the player will silently serve a wrong-looking video when it hits, so document the invariant either way. |
